<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\ingest\rf_resolver.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/rf_resolver.py

"""
Vectorized resolution of human-facing risk-factor name strings into the int32
Universe Indices (UIX) consumed by the compiled blocks and JIT kernels.

Sibling of `fx_resolver.py`: that module resolves FX-pair strings; this one
resolves single-name risk factors (underlyings, discount curves, price/vol
factors). Together they are the layer where "strings die early" — every name
column is collapsed to a flat C-contiguous np.int32 array before any data
leaves the ingest edge.

All resolution is performed through pandas `Series.map` (a C-level hash join).
There are no Python row loops, no `.apply`, and no regex matching.

Failure-mode contract
---------------------
Both `resolve_required_ids` and `resolve_optional_ids` HARD-FAIL on
populated-but-unmapped names. The optional path used to swallow these
silently via `.astype(np.float64)[...].astype(np.int32)` (NaN -> platform
MIN_INT32), corrupting the int32 ids array on typos. That silent path is
gone: `_detect_unmapped` now runs before any narrowing for both helpers.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.exceptions import PrettyKeyError

# A canonicalized string column uses the empty string as its missing sentinel
# (canonicalize.py maps NA -> "" on extraction). "" is never a valid RF name.
_BLANK: str = ""


def _detect_unmapped(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapped: pd.Series,
    blank_mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Raise on names that are populated but absent from the lookup table."""
    unmapped = mapped.isna().to_numpy(copy=False) & ~blank_mask
    if not unmapped.any():
        return

    bad = sorted({str(v) for v in names[unmapped]})[:10]
    raise PrettyKeyError(
        f"{ctx}: {int(unmapped.sum())} name(s) could not be resolved to a "
        f"risk-factor universe index.\nUnresolved names:\n{bad}"
    )


def resolve_required_ids(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapping: dict[str, int],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    Resolve a name column where every row MUST carry a mappable name.

    Parameters
    ----------
    names
        Flat string array (blanks are treated as errors here).
    mapping
        Name -> universe-index lookup table.
    ctx
        Human-readable column label for diagnostics.
    """
    blank_mask = names == _BLANK
    mapped = pd.Series(names).map(mapping)
    _detect_unmapped(names, mapped, blank_mask, ctx=ctx)

    if blank_mask.any():
        bad = np.flatnonzero(blank_mask)[:10].tolist()
        raise PrettyKeyError(f"{ctx}: required name missing at rows {bad}.")
    # No NaNs survive past the guards above -> direct int32 narrowing is safe.
    arr = np.ascontiguousarray(mapped.to_numpy(dtype=np.int32))
    return cast(npt.NDArray[np.int32], arr)


def resolve_optional_ids(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapping: Mapping[str, int | np.int32],
    *,
    sentinel: int | np.integer,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    Resolve a name column where blank rows are legitimately absent.

    Blank rows collapse to `sentinel`. A populated-but-unmapped name is a
    hard error: it signals a typo or a stale universe rather than absence.

    Parameters
    ----------
    names
        Flat string array; blank rows ("") map to `sentinel`.
    mapping
        Name -> universe-index lookup table.
    sentinel
        Typed sentinel (from riskquant.constants) for "no linkage".
    ctx
        Human-readable column label for diagnostics.

    Notes
    -----
    The detection-before-narrowing ordering matters: `mapped.to_numpy(
    dtype=np.float64)[populated].astype(np.int32)` on a NaN element returns
    `MIN_INT32` rather than raising — that path used to silently corrupt
    optional id columns on typos. Detection now runs unconditionally
    before any narrowing.
    """
    n = names.shape[0]
    blank_mask = names == _BLANK
    mapped = pd.Series(names).map(mapping)

    # Detect populated-but-unmapped BEFORE narrowing. After this gate the
    # only NaN-bearing rows in `mapped` are the blank ones, and those are
    # masked out of the scatter below.
    _detect_unmapped(names, mapped, blank_mask, ctx=ctx)

    # Edge allocation; downstream kernels never touch this array directly.
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    out.fill(np.int32(sentinel))

    populated = ~blank_mask
    if populated.any():
        # Safe int64 narrowing: `_detect_unmapped` guarantees no NaNs in
        # the populated subset. The float64 intermediate is gone — we go
        # straight through int64 to int32 to keep platform behaviour on
        # NaN coercion irrelevant.
        out[populated] = (
            mapped[populated].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64, copy=False).astype(np.int32)
        )

    return out  # np.empty is already C-contiguous
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/schema.py

"""
Structural ingest schema definitions.

This module defines the canonical structural ingest contract for RiskQuant
portfolio ingestion. It validates ONLY structural invariants (column presence,
uniqueness, allowed universe). Semantic invariants (value ranges, cross-column
logic, instrument correctness) belong in the validation dispatcher.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

# ==============================================================================
# CORE REQUIRED SCHEMA CONTRACT (REQUIRED FOR ALL INGEST BATCHES)
# ==============================================================================

UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "uid",
    "instrument",
)

# ==============================================================================
# DOMAIN COLUMN REGISTRIES (REFLECTING DIFFERENT RISK DOMAINS)
# ==============================================================================

# Market-risk-related columns. May not be required for all instruments.
MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "quantity",
    "underlying",
    "settlement_ccy",
)

# Credit-exposure linkage columns. Optional for all instruments.
CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("netting_set_id",)

# # JTD loss model-related columns. May not be required for all instruments.
# JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
#     "ead",
#     "pd",
#     "lgd",
# )

# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUMENT EXTENSION COLS
# ==============================================================================

MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "strike_price",
    "strike_ccy",
    "is_call",
    "expiry_date",
    "delivery_date",
    "settlement_date",
    "under_discount_curve",
    "strike_discount_curve",
    # Container-name pointer for the option's implied-vol source. Optional
    # at the structural layer (SPOT / FORWARD rows leave it None); the
    # OPTION family validator enforces non-null.
    "volatility_container_name",
)

# # `beta` is the single-factor copula loading consumed by the JTD block compilers.
# JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
#     "beta",
#     "systemic_factor",
#     "idiosyncratic_factor",
#     "chi_squared_factor",
# )

# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL STRUCTURAL REGISTRY
# ==============================================================================

ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS
    + CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
)

CANONICAL_COLUMN_ORDER: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS
    + CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
)

# ==============================================================================
# TYPE REGISTRIES
# ==============================================================================

STRING_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    # Market-specific string columns
    "instrument",
    "underlying",
    "strike_ccy",
    "settlement_ccy",
    "under_discount_curve",
    "strike_discount_curve",
    "volatility_container_name",
    # # JTD loss model-specific string columns
    # "systemic_factor",
    # "idiosyncratic_factor",
    # "chi_squared_factor",
)

FLOAT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    # Market-specific float columns
    "quantity",
    "strike_price",
    # # JTD loss model-specific float columns
    # "ead",
    # "pd",
    # "lgd",
    # "beta",
)

INT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("uid",)

# Nullable integer columns: exact integers, NA permitted (NA -> typed sentinel
# downstream). Distinct from INT_COLS, which are strictly non-nullable.
NULLABLE_INT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("netting_set_id",)

FLAG_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("is_call",)

DATE_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "delivery_date",
    "settlement_date",
    "expiry_date",
)

DATETIME_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ()

ALL_TYPED_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    STRING_COLS
    + FLOAT_COLS
    + INT_COLS
    + NULLABLE_INT_COLS
    + FLAG_COLS
    + DATE_COLS
    + DATETIME_COLS
)

if len(ALL_TYPED_COLS) != len(set(ALL_TYPED_COLS)):
    raise RuntimeError("Column appears in multiple dtype registries.")

ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(ALL_TYPED_COLS)

typed_missing = ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET - ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET
if typed_missing:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Allowed columns missing dtype registry:\n{sorted(typed_missing)}"
    )

illegal_typed = ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET - ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET
if illegal_typed:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Typed registry contains unknown columns:\n{sorted(illegal_typed)}"
    )

# ==============================================================================
# NON-NULLABLE REGISTRY
# ==============================================================================

NON_NULLABLE_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS

NON_NULLABLE_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(NON_NULLABLE_COLS)

unknown_non_nullable = NON_NULLABLE_COLS_SET - ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET
if unknown_non_nullable:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"NON_NULLABLE_COLS contains unknown cols:\n{sorted(unknown_non_nullable)}"
    )

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(ALL_TYPED_COLS)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/utils.py

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def is_na_helper(x: object) -> bool:
    """Check if a scalar value is missing or invalid (None, NaN, pd.NA, ±inf)."""
    if x is None:
        # None is considered missing.
        return True

    if x is pd.NA:
        # pd.NA is considered missing.
        return True

    if isinstance(x, (float, np.floating)):
        # Check for NaN or infinite values.
        # np.isfinite returns False for NaN and ±inf, so we return True for those cases.
        # NaNs and infinite values are considered missing/invalid.
        return not np.isfinite(x)

    # For all other types (e.g., int, str), we consider them valid and not missing.
    return False
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/validate.py

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.schema import STRING_COLS
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY,
    INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY,
    InstrumentTypeFamily,
)
from riskquant.utils.dataframes import validate_string_cols

# ==============================================================================
# INTERNAL HELPERS
# ==============================================================================


def _require_cols(df: pd.DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...]) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in cols if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"Missing required columns:\n{missing}")


def _validate_non_null_masked(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    cols: tuple[str, ...],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        arr = df[col].isna().to_numpy(copy=False)
        bad = mask & arr
        if np.any(bad):
            idx = df.index[bad].tolist()[:10]
            raise ValueError(f"{ctx} missing '{col}' at rows: {idx}")


def _validate_ccy_series(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    col: str,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    ser = df[col]
    valid_idx = np.flatnonzero(mask & ser.notna().to_numpy(copy=False))

    if len(valid_idx) == 0:
        return

    subset = ser.iloc[valid_idx]

    alpha = subset.str.isalpha()
    if not alpha.all():
        idx = subset.index[~alpha].tolist()[:10]
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx} invalid currency (non-alpha) at rows: {idx}")

    length = subset.str.len().eq(3)
    if not length.all():
        idx = subset.index[~length].tolist()[:10]
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx} invalid currency (len!=3) at rows: {idx}")


def _validate_non_zero_quantity(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    qty = df["quantity"].to_numpy(copy=False)

    with np.errstate(invalid="ignore"):
        dust = np.abs(qty) < POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE

    bad = mask & dust
    if np.any(bad):
        idx = df.index[bad].tolist()[:25]
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx} contain zero / dust quantities.\n"
            f"Tolerance={POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE}\nRows: {idx}"
        )


# ==============================================================================
# FAST CORE: INSTRUMENT → ID → FAMILY
# ==============================================================================


def _extract_instrument_id_array(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    ser = df["instrument"]

    id_arr = ser.map(INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY).to_numpy()

    if np.any(pd.isna(id_arr)):
        bad_mask = pd.isna(id_arr)
        idx = ser.index[bad_mask].tolist()[:10]
        vals = sorted(ser[bad_mask].dropna().unique().tolist())

        raise ValueError(f"Invalid instruments:\n{vals}\nRows: {idx}")

    return id_arr.astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def _derive_family_array(
    inst_id_arr: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.take(INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY, inst_id_arr)


def _mask_eq(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
    val: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    return arr == val


def _mask_in(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
    vals: tuple[int, ...],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    mask = np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=bool)
    for v in vals:
        mask |= arr == v
    return mask


# ==============================================================================
# FAMILY-LEVEL VALIDATION
# ==============================================================================


def validate_spot_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Spot")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Spot")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Spot")


def validate_forward_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
        "strike_price",
        "strike_ccy",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Forward")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Forward")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "strike_ccy", mask, "Forward")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Forward")


def validate_option_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
        "strike_price",
        "strike_ccy",
        "expiry_date",
        "is_call",
        # OPTION rows MUST point at a vol container; non-option rows
        # may leave the column None. The compiler resolves the name to
        # an int32 container id and derives the y-axis-type id off
        # `surface_meta_dict[id].y_axis_kind`.
        "volatility_container_name",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Option")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Option")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "strike_ccy", mask, "Option")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Option")


# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUMENT-LEVEL VALIDATION (SECONDARY DISPATCH)
# ==============================================================================

# ---> SPOT: N/A
# ---> VANILLA FORWARD NODE / CURVE: TODO
# ---> VANILLA EUROPEAN OPTION NODE / CURVE: TODO


# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL VALIDATORS
# ==============================================================================


def validate_unique_uids(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    ser = df["uid"]
    if not ser.is_unique:
        dupes = ser[ser.duplicated()].unique().tolist()
        raise ValueError(f"Duplicate UIDs:\n{dupes[:10]}")


# ==============================================================================
# MAIN ENTRYPOINT
# ==============================================================================


def validate_materialized_ingest_df(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """
    High-performance semantic validation.
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 1. Global
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    validate_unique_uids(df)
    validate_string_cols(df, STRING_COLS)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 2. Instrument → ID
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    inst_id = _extract_instrument_id_array(df)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 3. ID → Family
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    family = _derive_family_array(inst_id)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 4. Masks (pure NumPy)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    # --- FAMILY MASKS (PRIMARY DISPATCH) ---
    spot_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentTypeFamily.SPOT.value)
    fwd_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentTypeFamily.FORWARD.value)
    opt_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentTypeFamily.OPTION.value)

    # --- INSTRUMENT MASKS (SECONDARY DISPATCH) ---
    # (none yet, but this is where they'd go once we have any instrument-level validators)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 5. Validation passes
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    # --- FAMILY LAYER (PRIMARY DISPATCH) ---

    # Family layer
    validate_spot_family(df, spot_mask)
    validate_forward_family(df, fwd_mask)
    validate_option_family(df, opt_mask)

    # --- INSTRUMENT REFINEMENT (SECONDARY DISPATCH) ---
```

# src/riskquant/portfolio/instruments.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final

# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT (ABI-STABLE IDENTIFIERS)
# ============================================================


class InstrumentType(IntEnum):
    """
    Instrument = dispatch identifier for evaluation units.

    This includes:
    - tradable contracts (spot, forwards, options,bonds, swaps, etc)
    - stochastic loss processes (credit JTD)

    Behavior is defined by EvaluationTopology, NOT by instrument name.
    """

    SPOT = 0

    VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE = 1
    VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE = 2

    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE = 3
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE = 4

    VANILLA_IRS = 5


NUM_INSTRUMENTS: Final[int] = len(InstrumentType)


INSTRUMENT_TYPE_NAMES = frozenset(inst.name for inst in InstrumentType)

# ============================================================
# IDS FOR DISPATCH (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================

SPOT_ID: Final[int] = InstrumentType.SPOT.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID: Final[int] = InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE.value
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID: Final[int] = (
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE.value
)
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = (
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE.value
)
VANILLA_IRS_ID: Final[int] = InstrumentType.VANILLA_IRS.value


# ============================================================
# EVALUATION TOPOLOGY (PRIMARY DISPATCH AXIS)
# ============================================================


class EvaluationTopology(IntEnum):
    """
    Defines HOW an object is evaluated.
    This is the primary driver of execution strategy.
    """

    MTM = 0  # direct evaluation (spot, forwards)
    PATH_DEPENDENT = 1  # depends on full path
    OPTIMAL_STOPPING = 2  # requires backward induction


# ============================================================
# OPTIONAL: INSTRUMENT FAMILY (NON-DISPATCH)
# ============================================================


class InstrumentTypeFamily(IntEnum):
    SPOT = 0
    FORWARD = 1
    OPTION = 2
    RATES = 3
    CREDIT = 4


# ============================================================
# CORE REGISTRIES (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_EVALUATION_TOPOLOGY: Final[
    dict[InstrumentType, EvaluationTopology]
] = {
    # --- MTM ---
    InstrumentType.SPOT: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    # --- Options ---
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    # --- Interest Rate Swaps ---
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_IRS: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
}


INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY: Final[
    dict[InstrumentType, InstrumentTypeFamily]
] = {
    # SPOT
    InstrumentType.SPOT: InstrumentTypeFamily.SPOT,
    # FORWARDS
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: InstrumentTypeFamily.FORWARD,
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: InstrumentTypeFamily.FORWARD,
    # OPTIONS
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: InstrumentTypeFamily.OPTION,
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: InstrumentTypeFamily.OPTION,
    # RATES
    InstrumentType.VANILLA_IRS: InstrumentTypeFamily.RATES,
}


# ============================================================
# VALIDATION (HARD FAIL ON DRIFT)
# ============================================================


_ALL_INST_TYPES = frozenset(InstrumentType)

if frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_EVALUATION_TOPOLOGY) != _ALL_INST_TYPES:
    raise RuntimeError("Topology registry drift")

if frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY) != _ALL_INST_TYPES:
    raise RuntimeError("Family registry drift")


# ============================================================
# FAST DISPATCH ARRAYS (CRITICAL FOR PERFORMANCE)
# ============================================================


# Dense arrays for O(1) lookup without dicts
INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_TOPOLOGY_ARRAY: Final[tuple[int, ...]] = tuple(
    INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_EVALUATION_TOPOLOGY[InstrumentType(i)]
    for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
)

INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY: Final[tuple[int, ...]] = tuple(
    INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY[InstrumentType(i)]
    for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
)


# ============================================================
# EDGE (STRING) REGISTRIES — ONLY FOR I/O
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, InstrumentType]] = {
    inst.name: inst for inst in InstrumentType
}

INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, int]] = {
    inst.name: inst.value for inst in InstrumentType
}

INSTRUMENT_ID_TO_NAME_REGISTRY: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = tuple(
    InstrumentType(i).name for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
)


# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT FAMILY TO INSTRUMENT NAME REGISTRIES
# ============================================================


# Family → tuple of instrument names (all instruments in a family)
INSTRUMENT_FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[
    dict[InstrumentTypeFamily, tuple[str, ...]]
] = {
    family: tuple(
        inst.name
        for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY.items()
        if fam == family
    )
    for family in InstrumentTypeFamily
}

# Family → tuple of instrument IDs
INSTRUMENT_FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_IDS: Final[
    dict[InstrumentTypeFamily, tuple[int, ...]]
] = {
    family: tuple(
        inst.value
        for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY.items()
        if fam == family
    )
    for family in InstrumentTypeFamily
}

# Family name (string) → tuple of instrument names
INSTRUMENT_FAMILY_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]] = {
    family.name: tuple(
        inst.name
        for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY.items()
        if fam == family
    )
    for family in InstrumentTypeFamily
}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Instument.
    print("\nInstruments:")
    for inst in InstrumentType:
        print(f"  {inst.name} (ID={inst.value})")

    # Sanity check: print all registries
    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY:")
    for inst, topo in INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_EVALUATION_TOPOLOGY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {topo.name}")

    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY:")
    for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TYPE_TO_INSTRUMENT_FAMILY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {fam.name}")

    print("\nFAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES:")
    for fam, insts in INSTRUMENT_FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES.items():
        print(f"  {fam.name} → {insts}")

    print("\n --- END: ALL CHECKS PASSED --- \n")

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/validation.py
"""
Pre-compile validation of `IngestBatch` global-ID references against the
`UixMapper` universes.

Runs ONCE between universe discovery (`build_uix_mapper`) and block
compilation. Catches any global ID or ordinal that the upstream resolver
emitted but the universe-builder didn't see — i.e. a referential-integrity
bug somewhere in the ingest pipeline. By doing this before compile, the
hot-path `map_*_to_uix` calls can stay branch-light and trust their input.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterable, Sequence

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_membership
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.universe import UixMapper


def validate_ingest_batches_against_uix_universe(
    *,
    ingest_batches: Sequence[IngestBatch],
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> None:
    """
    Validate that every non-sentinel ID and ordinal referenced by the
    ingest batches is present in the corresponding `UixMapper` universe.

    Raises `ValueError` with the first batch of offending values it finds,
    grouped by universe.
    """
    # --- MARKET IDs -------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.underlying_ids,
                b.volatility_ids,
                b.fx_u2s_ids,
                b.fx_k2s_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.market_id_universe,
        label="market IDs",
    )

    # --- DISCOUNT CURVE IDs -----------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.under_discount_curve_ids,
                b.strike_discount_curve_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.discount_curve_id_universe,
        label="discount-curve IDs",
    )

    # --- DATES ------------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (b.settle_ord, b.delivery_ord, b.expiry_ord)
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.date_ord32_universe,
        label="ordinal dates",
    )


def _check_arrays_against_universe(
    *,
    arrays: Iterable[npt.NDArray[np.int32]],
    universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    label: str,
) -> None:
    """
    For every input array, assert that every value >= 0 also appears in
    `universe`. Vectorized: one `searchsorted` per input array, no Python
    loop over rows.
    """
    for arr in arrays:
        populated = arr[arr >= 0]
        if populated.size == 0:
            continue

        matches = searchsorted_membership(universe=universe, raw=populated)
        if not matches.all():
            missing = np.unique(populated[~matches])
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown {label} encountered during UIX validation: {missing}"
            )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\universe.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/universe.py
"""
Run-level UIX universe contract.

`UixMapper` is the immutable single source of truth for global-ID -> UIX
translation across a run. It is deliberately a LEAF module: it depends only
on `_arrays` and `constants`, so any tier (portfolio, market_data, simlab)
can import the contract type at runtime without a layering inversion. The
PRODUCER, `build_uix_mapper(ingest_batches)`, stays in `portfolio.engine`
because it consumes portfolio internals (`IngestBatch`); only the contract
type lives here.

This module was extracted from `portfolio.engine` (item C2) specifically to
let `market_data.builder` / `market_data.assemble` drop their `TYPE_CHECKING`
deferral of `UixMapper` — the deferral existed only to dodge the
`market_data -> portfolio` import cycle, which no longer exists now that the
contract type is a leaf.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32
from riskquant.constants import INVALID_UIX


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class UixMapper:
    """
    Immutable execution-space translator.

    This is the single source of truth for global-ID → UIX translation.

    Holds the four global universes touched by the run plus the deduplicated
    FX-effective pair table. All universe arrays are sorted ascending and
    unique; the `__post_init__` validation runs once so the runtime `map_*`
    methods can stay allocation-light and branch-light.

    Field layout
    ------------
    market_id_universe          : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by
                                  prices / forwards / vols / fx-spots.
                                  Indexed by `price_uix`, `vol_uix`,
                                  `underlying_price_curve_uix`, etc.
    discount_curve_id_universe  : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by discount
                                  curves. Kept distinct from `market_id_universe`
                                  because the discount-curve ID range is
                                  disjoint and `MarketDataMatrix.discount_factor_grid`
                                  has its own axis-0 universe.
    date_ord32_universe         : sorted-unique int32 day-ordinals
                                  (days-since-epoch) touched by the run.
                                  Indexed by `*_date_uix` on blocks.
    fx_effective_pairs          : (K, 2) int32 deduplicated `(fx_rf_uix, invert)`
                                  table. `fx_rf_uix` is itself an index into
                                  `market_id_universe`; `invert` is 0 or 1.
                                  The market-data builder turns this into a
                                  `(K, S)` `fx_spots_effective` matrix.
    """

    market_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    discount_curve_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    date_ord32_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    fx_effective_pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # --- INVARIANTS (CHECKED ONCE) -----------------------------------------

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._validate_universe(self.market_id_universe, "market_id_universe")
        self._validate_universe(
            self.discount_curve_id_universe, "discount_curve_id_universe"
        )
        self._validate_universe(self.date_ord32_universe, "date_ord32_universe")
        self._validate_fx_effective_pairs(self.fx_effective_pairs)

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_universe(arr: npt.NDArray[np.int32], name: str) -> None:
        if arr.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"{name} must be int32; got {arr.dtype}.")
        if arr.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be 1-D; got ndim={arr.ndim}.")
        if not arr.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be C-contiguous.")
        if arr.size > 1 and np.any(arr[1:] <= arr[:-1]):
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be strictly increasing and unique.")

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_fx_effective_pairs(pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]) -> None:
        if pairs.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"fx_effective_pairs must be int32; got {pairs.dtype}.")
        if pairs.ndim != 2 or (pairs.size and pairs.shape[1] != 2):
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx_effective_pairs must have shape (K, 2); got {pairs.shape}"
            )
        if not pairs.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError("fx_effective_pairs must be C-contiguous.")

    # --- TRANSLATION API ---------------------------------------------------

    def map_market_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global market IDs into `market_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global discount-curve IDs into
        `discount_curve_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.discount_curve_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_dates_to_uix(
        self,
        ordinals: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global day-ordinals into `date_ord32_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`ordinals < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.date_ord32_universe,
            raw=ordinals,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        self,
        *,
        raw_fx_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        raw_fx_inv: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate per-trade `(raw_fx_id, raw_fx_inv)` into per-trade
        `fx_spots_effective_uix` indices.

        Steps (all vectorized, no Python loop):
          1. Translate `raw_fx_ids` into `market_id_universe` UIXs.
          2. Look the resulting `(fx_uix, invert)` pair up in
             `fx_effective_pairs` to obtain the effective UIX.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_fx_ids == NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        if raw_fx_ids.shape != raw_fx_inv.shape:
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx legs shape mismatch: ids={raw_fx_ids.shape}, "
                f"inv={raw_fx_inv.shape}"
            )

        # Stage 1: market-id universe lookup. Sentinels collapse to INVALID_UIX.
        fx_market_uix = searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_fx_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

        # Stage 2: dedup-pair lookup. We pack each (uix, inv) pair into a
        # single int64 key for a vectorized one-shot searchsorted into the
        # equivalently-packed effective-pairs table.
        n = raw_fx_ids.shape[0]
        out = np.full(n, INVALID_UIX, dtype=np.int32)

        if self.fx_effective_pairs.size == 0:
            return out

        needs_fx = fx_market_uix != INVALID_UIX
        if not needs_fx.any():
            return out

        needed_uix = fx_market_uix[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_inv = raw_fx_inv[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_key = (needed_uix << 1) | needed_inv

        pair_uix = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 0].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_inv = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 1].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_key = (pair_uix << 1) | pair_inv

        idx = np.searchsorted(pair_key, needed_key)
        # Every needed_key MUST exist in pair_key (it was deduplicated from
        # exactly this input). Validate to fail loud on contract drift.
        if idx.size and (
            idx.max() >= pair_key.size or np.any(pair_key[idx] != needed_key)
        ):
            raise RuntimeError(
                "FX-effective dedup contract violated: per-trade pair not "
                "found in fx_effective_pairs. Mapper was built from a "
                "different batch than this one."
            )

        out[needs_fx] = idx.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
        return out
```

```python
from pandas import DataFrame
from pandas.api.types import is_string_dtype


def validate_string_cols(df: DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...] | list[str]) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        if col not in df.columns:
            continue

        s = df[col]

        if not is_string_dtype(s):
            raise ValueError(f"Column '{col}' must be of string dtype.")

        non_na = s.dropna()
        if non_na.empty:
            continue

        # Length
        max_len = non_na.str.len().max()
        if max_len > 255:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains strings longer than 255 characters "
                f"(max={max_len})."
            )

        # Canonical format
        pattern = r"^[A-Z0-9]+(?:_[A-Z0-9]+)*(?:\[(?:[A-Z]+[+-]\d+|(?:ON|TN|SN|\d+[DWMY]))\])?$"
        if not non_na.str.match(pattern).all():
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains non-canonical strings. "
                f"Expected format: UPPERCASE tokens separated by underscores."
            )
```

```python
# riskquant/utils/dates.py
from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
from collections.abc import Iterable
from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import DATE_EPOCH

DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D: Final[np.datetime64] = np.datetime64(
    DATE_EPOCH.isoformat(),
    "D",
)


def coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(
    as_of_date: pd.Timestamp | dt.date | pd.Timestamp | str,
) -> pd.Timestamp:
    """
    Accepts: pd.Timestamp | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | str
    Returns: pd.Timestamp (tz-naive)
    """
    # If str, validate format.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, str):
        assert_date_str_is_iso_format(as_of_date)

    # As pd.Timestamp
    ts = pd.Timestamp(as_of_date)

    # If caller passed a date (no time), treat it as end-of-day inclusive
    # so 2026-01-02 includes all times on 2026-01-02.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, dt.date) and not isinstance(as_of_date, dt.datetime):
        ts = ts + pd.Timedelta(days=1) - pd.Timedelta(nanoseconds=1)  # type: ignore[operator]  # mypy doesn't know Timestamp supports this math

    # Normalize tz handling (if any). Adjust policy as you like.
    if ts.tzinfo is not None:
        ts = ts.tz_convert(None)

    return ts


def is_date_str_valid(date_str: str) -> bool:
    """Checks if date_str which must have form %Y-%m-%d is a valid calendar date."""
    if not isinstance(date_str, str):
        return False
    if len(date_str) != 10 or date_str[4] != "-" or date_str[7] != "-":
        return False
    if not date_str.replace("-", "").isdigit():
        return False

    try:
        pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise")
    except ValueError:
        return False

    return True


def assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str: str) -> None:
    """Assert that date_str is in ISO format 'YYYY-MM-DD'."""
    if not is_date_str_valid(date_str):
        raise ValueError(f"Date string '{date_str}' is not in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format.")


def date_str_to_date(date_str: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert a date string in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format to a datetime.date object."""
    assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str)
    return pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise").date()


def date_to_ord32(d: dt.date) -> np.int32:
    return np.int32((d - DATE_EPOCH).days)


def ordinal32_to_date(days: int) -> dt.date:
    return DATE_EPOCH + pd.Timedelta(days=int(days))  # or datetime.timedelta


def datetime64D_to_ord32(dts: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert numpy datetime64[D] array -> int32 days since DATE_EPOCH.
    Returns a view/copy depending on input; no pandas involvement.
    """
    dts = np.asarray(dts, dtype="datetime64[D]")
    return (dts - DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def ordinal32_to_datetime64D(days: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert int32 days since DATE_EPOCH -> numpy datetime64[D] array.
    """
    days = np.asarray(days, dtype=np.int32)
    # Cast to timedelta64[D] then add epoch
    return DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D + days.astype("timedelta64[D]", copy=False)


def ym_str_to_int_tuple(ym: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Convert 'YYYYMM' -> (YYYY, MM)."""
    if len(ym) != 6 or not ym.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    y, m = int(ym[:4]), int(ym[4:6])
    if not 1 <= m <= 12:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    return y, m


def ymd_str_to_date(ymd: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert 'YYYYMMDD' -> datetime.date."""
    if len(ymd) != 8 or not ymd.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YMD string: {ymd}")
    return dt.date(int(ymd[:4]), int(ymd[4:6]), int(ymd[6:8]))


def month_diff(
    *,
    anchor_ym: tuple[int, int],
    target_ym: tuple[int, int],
) -> int:
    """Difference in months: target - anchor."""
    ay, am = anchor_ym
    ty, tm = target_ym
    return (ty - ay) * 12 + (tm - am)


def next_half_year_boundary(
    t0: dt.date,
    ym_boundaries: Iterable[str] = ("0630", "1231"),
) -> dt.date:
    """
    Return the first boundary date >= t0 among {YYYY0630, YYYY1231} (or configured boundaries).
    If none remain in the year, return the first boundary in the next year.
    """
    ym_bounds = list(ym_boundaries)
    if not ym_bounds:
        raise ValueError("No half-year boundaries provided.")

    year = t0.year
    candidates = []
    for b in ym_bounds:
        if len(b) != 4 or not b.isdigit():
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid year-month boundary 'MMDD': {b}")
        m = int(b[:2])
        d = int(b[2:])
        candidates.append(dt.date(year, m, d))

    for d in sorted(candidates):
        if d >= t0:
            return d

    # none left this year -> first boundary next year (sorted)
    year += 1
    b0 = sorted(ym_bounds)[0]
    return dt.date(year, int(b0[:2]), int(b0[2:]))


def half_year_index(d: dt.date) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """
    Map boundary date to a half-year index:
      YYYY-06-30 -> (YYYY, 0)
      YYYY-12-31 -> (YYYY, 1)
    """
    if (d.month, d.day) == (6, 30):
        return d.year, 0
    if (d.month, d.day) == (12, 31):
        return d.year, 1
    raise ValueError(f"Not a recognized half-year boundary: {d.isoformat()}")


def half_year_diff(
    *,
    anchor: dt.date,
    target: dt.date,
) -> int:
    """Difference in half-years: target - anchor, where each boundary step counts as 1."""
    ay, ah = half_year_index(anchor)
    ty, th = half_year_index(target)
    return (ty - ay) * 2 + (th - ah)


def get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> str:
    # Ensure holidays is a list of strings.
    if holiday_dates is None:
        holiday_dates = []
    if isinstance(holiday_dates, tuple):
        holiday_dates = list(holiday_dates)
    if not isinstance(holiday_dates, list):
        raise TypeError("holiday_dates must be a list!")

    # Create a custom business day with the holidays.
    custom_business_day = pd.offsets.CustomBusinessDay(holidays=holiday_dates)

    # Start from today and apply the lag and one additional business day.
    start_date = dt.datetime.today().date()
    adjusted_date = start_date - custom_business_day * (lag_in_bus_days + 1)
    date_as_str = adjusted_date.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

    # Append the time string if provided, and return.
    if time_str_to_append_to_date is not None:
        return date_as_str + f" {time_str_to_append_to_date}"
    return date_as_str


def get_previous_business_date_as_date(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> dt.date:
    # Get as string.
    date_string = get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
        lag_in_bus_days=lag_in_bus_days,
        holiday_dates=holiday_dates,
        time_str_to_append_to_date=time_str_to_append_to_date,
    )

    # To date.
    return dt.date.fromisoformat(date_string)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/utils/printer.py
from __future__ import annotations

import math
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

# ==========================================
# DEFAULT STYLING & ANSI COLORS
# ==========================================
ANSI_CYAN = "\033[96m"
ANSI_GREEN = "\033[92m"
ANSI_FOREST_GREEN = "\033[38;5;28m"  # A rich, medium-dark green (#008700)
ANSI_DEEP_GREEN = "\033[38;5;22m"  # A very dark, subtle green (#005f00)
ANSI_YELLOW = "\033[93m"
ANSI_RED = "\033[91m"
ANSI_MAGENTA = "\033[95m"
ANSI_BOLD = "\033[1m"
ANSI_RESET = "\033[0m"
ANSI_BLUE = "\033[34m"  # Standard Dark/Deep Blue
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLUE = (
    "\033[94m"  # High-intensity Blue (matches the brightness of your other variables)
)
ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE = "\033[38;5;17m"  # Deepest dark blue (#00005f)
ANSI_NAVY_BLUE = "\033[38;5;18m"  # Slightly brighter navy (#000087)

ANSI_BLACK = "\033[30m"
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLACK = "\033[90m"  # Dark gray, great for subdued metadata
ANSI_BLACK_BG = "\033[40m"


DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR = ANSI_BLUE


def print_header(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╭{'─' * width}╮{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}│ {title_} │{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╰{'─' * width}╯{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┏{'━' * width}┓{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┃ {title_} ┃{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┗{'━' * width}┛{ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_subheader(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}  {title_}  {ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD} {'─' * width} {ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_text(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
) -> None:
    import textwrap

    if add_space_before:
        print()
    print(textwrap.fill(text, width=width))
    if add_space_after:
        print()


def as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> str:
    # Convert an iterable of items to a nicely formatted string with each
    # item on its own line, prefixed by "- ".
    items = (str(i) for i in x)  # generator avoids extra allocations
    return "- " + ",\n- ".join(items) + "."


def print_as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> None:
    print(as_list_str(x))


def print_wrapped_text_box(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 1,
    corner_char: str = "+",
    top_border: str = "-",
    bottom_border: str = "-",
    horiz_border: str = "|",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Word‑wrap `text` to `width` characters and display it in an ASCII box."""
    import textwrap

    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)

    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2
    horiz = corner_char + top_border * inner_width + corner_char

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    print(f"{c}{bold}{horiz}{reset}")
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
        )
    print(f"{c}{bold}{corner_char + bottom_border * inner_width + corner_char}{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_success_message(
    message: str | None = None,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    uppercase: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_GREEN,
) -> None:
    """
    Print a success message inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import textwrap

    s = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    if message:
        s += f" | {message}"
    else:
        s += " | SUCCESS! CODE RAN SUCCESSFULLY!"

    text = s.upper() if uppercase else s.capitalize()

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_calling_file(
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_BLACK,
) -> None:
    """
    Print the name of the Python file that called this function
    inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import inspect
    import os
    import textwrap

    frame = inspect.currentframe()
    try:
        caller_frame = frame.f_back if frame else None
        filename = caller_frame.f_code.co_filename if caller_frame else "<unknown>"
        filename = os.path.basename(filename)
    finally:
        # Explicitly delete frame references to avoid reference cycles
        del frame

    dt_str = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    text = f"{dt_str} | RUNNING FILE: {filename}"

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict(
    d: dict[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating]
    | Mapping[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    indent: int = 0,
    indent_step: int = 4,
    sort_keys: bool = False,
    limit: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Pretty-print a dictionary with aligned keys and nested support."""
    items = sorted(d.items()) if sort_keys else list(d.items())
    if limit is not None:
        items = items[:limit]

    try:
        max_key_len = max(len(str(k)) for k in d)
    except ValueError:
        max_key_len = 0

    pad = " " * indent

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    print(pad + "{")
    for key, value in items:
        key_str = str(key)
        key_pad = key_str.ljust(max_key_len)

        if isinstance(value, (Mapping, dict)):
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}:")
            print_dict(
                d=value,
                indent=indent + indent_step * 2,
                indent_step=indent_step,
                sort_keys=sort_keys,
                limit=None,
                add_space_before=False,
                add_space_after=False,
            )
        else:
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}: {value}")

    print(pad + "}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


class _FloatFormatter:
    __slots__ = ("_fmt", "_zero_tol")

    def __init__(self, spec: str, zero_tol: float = 0.0) -> None:
        if "_" in spec:
            clean_spec = spec.replace("_", ",")
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=clean_spec: f"{x:{s}}".replace(",", "_")
        else:
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=spec: f"{x:{s}}"
        self._zero_tol = float(zero_tol)

    def __call__(self, x: float) -> str:
        if x is None or (isinstance(x, float) and math.isnan(x)):
            # Handle NaN alignment for Numpy formats
            try:
                width = int(
                    self._fmt.__defaults__[0]
                    .split(".")[0]
                    .replace(">", "")
                    .replace("<", "")
                )
                return "nan".rjust(width)
            except (ValueError, TypeError, IndexError):
                return "nan"
        if self._zero_tol and abs(x) < self._zero_tol:
            x = 0.0
        try:
            return self._fmt(x)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            return str(x)


def print_df(
    obj: pd.Series | pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a Series/DataFrame with optional display controls."""
    if isinstance(obj, pd.Series):
        name = obj.name if obj.name is not None else "value"
        df = obj.to_frame(name=name)
    else:
        df = obj

    if float_format is not None:
        numeric_cols = df.select_dtypes(
            include=["int64", "int32", "float64", "float32"]
        ).columns
        df = df.copy()
        df[numeric_cols] = df[numeric_cols].astype(float)

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        df_attrs = f"(shape={df.shape})"
        print_header(
            title=f"DataFrame: {title} {df_attrs}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is None:
        print(df.to_string(index=index, max_rows=max_rows, max_cols=max_cols))
    else:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print(
            df.to_string(
                index=index,
                max_rows=max_rows,
                max_cols=max_cols,
                float_format=formatter,
            )
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict_of_dfs(
    d: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    cols_before_transpose: int = 20,
    title_color: str | None = ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE,
    subtitle_color: str | None = ANSI_BLUE,
) -> None:
    """Print a dictionary of DataFrames with optional display controls."""
    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        # Use a slightly different color for the main dictionary wrapper if desired,
        # but defaulting to standard title behavior.
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary of DataFrames: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
        )

    for key, df in d.items():
        if df.shape[1] > cols_before_transpose:
            df = df.T
            key += " (transposed)"
        print_df(
            df,
            title=key,
            index=index,
            max_rows=max_rows,
            max_cols=max_cols,
            float_format=float_format,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
            zero_tol=zero_tol,
            title_color=subtitle_color,
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_array(
    arr: np.ndarray,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = ">10.2f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a NumPy array with perfectly aligned tabular columns and ANSI colors."""
    _array_info = f"(shape={arr.shape}, dtype={arr.dtype})"

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        full_title = f"Array: {title} {_array_info}"
        print_header(
            title=full_title,
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is not None and np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.integer):
        arr_to_print = arr.astype(float)
    else:
        arr_to_print = arr

    print_opts = {
        "suppress": True,
        "linewidth": 200,
    }

    if max_rows is not None or max_cols is not None:
        r_edge = max_rows // 2 if max_rows is not None else 3
        c_edge = max_cols // 2 if max_cols is not None else 3
        print_opts["edgeitems"] = max(r_edge, c_edge)

        row_limit = max_rows if max_rows is not None else arr_to_print.shape[0]
        col_limit = (
            max_cols
            if max_cols is not None
            else (arr_to_print.shape[1] if arr_to_print.ndim > 1 else 1)
        )
        print_opts["threshold"] = row_limit * col_limit - 1

    if float_format is not None:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print_opts["formatter"] = {"float_kind": formatter}

    with np.printoptions(**print_opts):
        print(arr_to_print)

    if add_space_after:
        print()
```
</file>

```python
# riskquant/utils/tenors.py
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date
from typing import Optional, Tuple


def parse_bracket_token(tenor_token: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[int]]:
    """
    "[Y-2]" -> ("Y", -2)
    "[M+0]" -> ("M", 0)
    "[D+24]" -> ("D", 24)
    "[HY+1]" -> ("HY", 1)
    "[ON]" -> ("ON", None)   (LABEL)
    """
    # Error.
    if tenor_token is None:
        raise ValueError("tenor_token cannot be None!")

    # Strip white space.
    s = tenor_token.strip()

    # Must start with '[' and end with ']'.
    if s.startswith("[") and s.endswith("]"):
        s = s[1:-1].strip()

    # Find the plus or minus sign.
    plus = s.rfind("+")
    minus = s.rfind("-")
    sign_pos = max(plus, minus)

    # If no sign.
    if sign_pos <= 0:
        return s, None

    # Unit (Y, M, D, HY, etc.)
    unit = s[:sign_pos]

    # Signed offset.
    offset = int(s[sign_pos:])

    # Return.
    return unit, offset


def get_spot_year(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    roll_month: int,
    roll_day: int,
    post_roll_shift: int,
) -> int:
    """
    Defines which delivery year corresponds to Y+0 under ROLL_ON_MMDD.
    """

    # Get the roll date,
    roll_date = date(as_of_date.year, roll_month, roll_day)

    # Compare to as-of-date.
    # As-of-date is on or after the roll date.
    if as_of_date >= roll_date:
        return (
            as_of_date.year + post_roll_shift
        )  # Current year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).

    # As-of-date is before the roll date.
    # Previous year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).
    return (as_of_date.year - 1) + post_roll_shift


def add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01(
    *,
    d: date,
    months_to_add: int,
) -> date:
    """Add months to a month-start date (day=1)."""
    y = d.year + (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) // 12
    m = (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) % 12 + 1
    return date(y, m, 1)


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyy(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymm(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymmdd(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}{d.day:02d}"
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/base_block.py

"""
Base class for all Struct-of-Arrays (SoA) trade blocks.

`Block` carries only what is universal to every compiled block: the flat int64
`trade_uids` array and the `BLOCK_TYPE_ID` dispatch tag. All economic SoA arrays
live on the concrete subclasses.

`BLOCK_TYPE_ID` is the single integer the valuation engine dispatches on: it
indexes directly into `BLOCK_EVALUATORS`, so no `type()` calls, no isinstance
ladders, no string keys. The base value is the `UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL`
sentinel; every concrete block MUST override it with its `BlockType` enum id.
The override is enforced at class-creation time by `__init_subclass__`, which
fails loudly the moment a new block type is added without wiring its id — a
compile-time guard rather than a runtime surprise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
from typing import Any, ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.blocks.block_type import (
    UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL,
    VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class Block:
    """
    Universal base for compiled SoA valuation blocks.

    Every array element of every field corresponds to the same row index.
    Concrete subclasses define the economic SoA fields required by their
    valuation kernel.

    `trade_uids[i]` identifies the originating trade that produced row `i` of the block.
    """

    # Block type identifier for dispatching to the right pricer kernel. Every concrete
    # subclass MUST override this with its `BlockType` enum id, which is
    # guaranteed to be unique.
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL

    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    def __init_subclass__(cls) -> None:
        super().__init_subclass__()

        if cls.__name__.startswith("_"):
            return

        if cls.BLOCK_TYPE_ID == UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL:
            raise TypeError(f"{cls.__name__} must define BLOCK_TYPE_ID")

        if cls.BLOCK_TYPE_ID not in VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{cls.__name__} has invalid BLOCK_TYPE_ID {cls.BLOCK_TYPE_ID}"
            )

    @property
    def size(self) -> int:
        """
        Number of elements in a block, i.e. number of cashflows, legs, or
        trades it represents.
        """
        return self.trade_uids.shape[0]

    def print_block(
        self,
        *,
        max_items: int | None = 10,
        float_precision: int = 4,
        sep: str = ", ",
        pad: int = 15,  # Increased default pad slightly to account for the new '_' characters
        thousand_sep: str = "_",
    ) -> None:
        """Print every SoA field with shape, dtype, and a value preview."""

        print(f"{type(self).__name__}:")
        for f in fields(self):
            v = getattr(self, f.name)
            if isinstance(v, np.ndarray):
                # Replace lambdas with typed inner functions to satisfy Pyright/MyPy
                def fmt_float(x: Any) -> str:
                    return f"{x:>{pad}{thousand_sep}.{float_precision}f}"

                def fmt_int(x: Any) -> str:
                    # Use 'd' (decimal integer) instead of a hacked float format
                    return f"{x:>{pad}{thousand_sep}d}"

                # Format the array preview cleanly
                val_preview = np.array2string(
                    v[:max_items],
                    separator=sep,
                    max_line_width=100_000,
                    formatter={
                        "float_kind": fmt_float,
                        "int_kind": fmt_int,
                    },
                )

                print(
                    f"  {f.name:<33} | shape: {v.shape!s:>10} | "
                    f"dtype: {v.dtype!s:>10} | values: {val_preview}"
                )
            else:
                print(f"  {f.name:<33} {v}")
        print()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\blocks\block_type.py">
```python
"""
Compiled valuation primitives (Block Types).

These are the executable, structure-of-arrays (SoA) blocks consumed
by the valuation engine.  They are **not** economic instruments.

Examples
--------
VANILLA_IRS
    ├── FIXED_CASHFLOW
    └── FLOATING_CASHFLOW

FIXED_RATE_BOND
    ├── FIXED_CASHFLOW
    └── PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW

FRN
    ├── FLOATING_CASHFLOW
    └── PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW
"""

from enum import IntEnum
from types import MappingProxyType
from typing import Final

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sentinel value for an explicitly unset / missing block type ID.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL: Final[int] = -999


class BlockType(IntEnum):
    """
    Executable SoA block primitives.

    Each member carries a stable integer ID used for serialisation,
    dispatch tables, and AoS ↔ SoA conversion.
    """

    # ======================================================================
    # MARKET RISK PRIMITIVES
    # ======================================================================
    SPOT = 0

    VANILLA_FORWARD = 1

    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION = 2

    # ======================================================================
    # INTEREST-RATE CASHFLOW PRIMITIVES
    # ======================================================================
    FIXED_CASHFLOW = 3
    FLOATING_CASHFLOW = 4
    PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW = 5

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Convenience constructors
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    @classmethod
    def from_id(cls, id_: int) -> "BlockType":
        """Return the member whose ``value`` equals *id_*."""
        if id_ not in BLOCK_TYPE_ID_TO_NAME:  # pragma: no cover
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid BlockType id: {id_}")
        # IntEnum allows instantiation directly from the integer value.
        return cls(id_)

    @classmethod
    def from_name(cls, name: str) -> "BlockType":
        """Return the member whose ``name`` equals *name* (case-sensitive)."""
        if name not in BLOCK_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID:  # pragma: no cover
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid BlockType name: {name!r}")
        return cls[name]


# ==========================================================================
# CONVENIENCE ID CONSTANTS FOR DISPATCH / LOOKUP
# ==========================================================================

SPOT_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.SPOT.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.VANILLA_FORWARD.value
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION.value
FIXED_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.FIXED_CASHFLOW.value
FLOATING_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.FLOATING_CASHFLOW.value
PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID: int = BlockType.PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW.value

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Immutable lookup tables (single source of truth).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

NUM_BLOCK_TYPES: Final[int] = len(BlockType)

BLOCK_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID: Final[MappingProxyType[str, int]] = MappingProxyType(
    {member.name: member.value for member in BlockType}
)

BLOCK_TYPE_ID_TO_NAME: Final[MappingProxyType[int, str]] = MappingProxyType(
    {member.value: member.name for member in BlockType}
)

# Frozen sets for fast membership tests / validation.
VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS: Final[frozenset[int]] = frozenset(BLOCK_TYPE_ID_TO_NAME.keys())
VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_NAMES: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(BLOCK_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID.keys())

# Guard: the sentinel must never collide with a real block type.
assert UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL not in VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS, (
    "Sentinel collides with a valid block-type ID"
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Light-weight demo / introspection (kept intentionally simple).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("\n--- BlockType Enumeration ---\n")
    for block_type in BlockType:
        print(f"{block_type.name:40s}  id = {block_type.value:>2d}")

    print("\nID → Name mapping:")
    print(BLOCK_TYPE_ID_TO_NAME)

    print("\nName → ID mapping:")
    print(BLOCK_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID)

    print("\nValid BlockType IDs:")
    print(VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS)

    print("\nValid BlockType Names:")
    print(VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_NAMES)

    print("\nSentinel value for unset block type ID:")
    print(UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL)

    assert UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL not in VALID_BLOCK_TYPE_IDS, (
        "Sentinel collides with a valid block-type ID"
    )

    print("\nAll checks passed: sentinel does not collide with valid block type IDs.")
    print("\n----------------------------\n")
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/discounted_cashflow_block.py
"""
Compiled blocks for discounted cashflows.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.blocks._base_block import Block
from riskquant.valuation.blocks.block_type import (
    UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL,
    BlockType,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _DiscountedCashflowBlock(Block):
    """Common SoA fields shared by all discounted cashflow blocks."""

    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = UNSET_BLOCK_TYPE_ID_SENTINEL

    # --- LINKAGES (UIX) ---

    # Index into `ValuationState.effective_discount_factors`.
    # Discounts cashflow to t = 0 and converts to settlement currency
    # at t = 0 in one step.
    effective_discount_factor_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class PrincipalCashflowBlock(_DiscountedCashflowBlock):
    """
    Compiled deterministic principal cashflows.

    CashFlow(T) = Amount

    PV(0) = CashFlow(T) * DiscountFactor(0, T) * FxSpot(0)

    PV(0) = CashFlow(T) * EffectiveDiscountFactor(0, T)

    Examples
    --------
    - Bond redemption
    - Deposit repayment
    - Cross-currency swap notional exchange
    - Bullet loan principal repayment
    - Final notional return on an FRN
    """

    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(BlockType.PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW)

    # --- TRADE SPECIFICS ---
    amount: npt.NDArray[np.float64]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class FixedCashflowBlock(_DiscountedCashflowBlock):
    """
    Compiled fixed-rate cashflows.

    CashFlow(T) = Notional * AccrualFactor * FixedRate * PayReceiveMult

    PV(0) = CashFlow(T) * DiscountFactor(0, T) * FxSpot(0)

    PV(0) = CashFlow(T) * EffectiveDiscountFactor(0, T)

    """

    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(BlockType.FIXED_CASHFLOW)

    # --- TRADE SPECIFICS ---

    notional: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    accrual_factor: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    fixed_rate: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    pay_receive_mult: npt.NDArray[np.int8]  # pay=-1, receive=+1


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class FloatingCashflowBlock(_DiscountedCashflowBlock):
    """
    Compiled floating-rate cashflows.

    FloatingRate(0, s, T) = ForwardRate(0, s, T) + Spread

    CashFlow(T) = Notional * FloatingRate(0, s, T) * AccrualFactor * PayReceiveMult

    PV(0) = CashFlow(T) * DiscountFactor(0, T) * FxSpot(0)
    """

    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(BlockType.FLOATING_CASHFLOW)

    # --- TRADE SPECIFICS ---

    notional: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    accrual_factor: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    spread: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    pay_receive_mult: npt.NDArray[np.int8]  # pay=-1, receive=+1

    # --- LINKAGES (UIX) ---

    # Index into `ValuationState.forward_rates` for the forward rate
    # underlying this cashflow.
    forward_rate_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
```

"""
Compiled, memory-efficient container for spot positions.

`SpotBlock` is the struct-of-arrays representation of a set of spot trades.
The block is intentionally compact (NumPy arrays, primitive numeric dtypes)
so it can be held efficiently in memory and passed between processes in
high-throughput valuation workflows.

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.blocks._base_block import Block
from riskquant.valuation.blocks.block_type import BlockType


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)

    """Struct-of-arrays for compiled spot trades."""

    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(BlockType.SPOT)

    # --- TRADE SPECIFICS ---

    # Signed quantities / notionals for the spot positions.
    qty: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # --- LINKAGES (UIX) ---

    # Index into `ValuationState.effective_spot_prices` for this row.
    effective_spot_price_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/vanilla_european_option_block.py
"""
Compiled blocks for vanilla European option trades.

Architectural contract
----------------------
* Row `i` of every array corresponds to the trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.
* Every `_uix` field is a zero-based, contiguous execution index produced by
  the portfolio engine's `UixMapper` — NOT a global static-config ID.
* FX legs are carried as `fx_*_to_settle_effective_uix` indexing
  `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`. No invert flags survive into the
  block layer.
* `cp_mult` packs `is_call` into `+1` / `-1` for branchless Black-76 payoff.
* `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in any optional `_uix` field signals "no factor
  required for this trade" and is the only branch the pricer kernel
  performs (hoisted outside the scenario loop).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
from numpy.typing import NDArray

from riskquant.valuation.blocks._base_block import Block
from riskquant.valuation.blocks.block_type import BlockType


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class VanillaEuropeanOptionBlock(Block):
    # --- BLOCK ID FOR DISPATCH ---
    BLOCK_TYPE_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(BlockType.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION)

    # --- TRADE SPECIFICS ---
    qty: NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: NDArray[np.float64]
    log_strike: NDArray[np.float64]
    cp_mult: NDArray[np.int8]
    sqrt_tte: NDArray[np.float64]
    half_tte: NDArray[np.float64]

    # --- LINKAGES (UIX) ---

    # Index into ValuationState.forward_prices for the underlying forward price in
    # the STRIKE CURRENCY. This is the forward price at the expiry date of the option.
    forward_price_strike_ccy_uix: NDArray[np.int32]

    # Index into ValuationState.log_forward_prices for the log of the underlying
    # forward price in the STRIKE CURRENCY. This is the log of the forward price
    # in STRIKE CURRENCY at the expiry date of the option.
    option_log_forward_price_strike_ccy_uix: NDArray[np.int32]

    # Index into ValuationState.vols for the volatility of the underlying forward price.
    # This is the volatility at the expiry date of the option.
    vol_uix: NDArray[np.int32]

    # Index into ValuationState.effective_discount_factors for the discount factor in
    # the STRIKE CURRENCY. Discounts the cashflow from the SETTLEMENT DATE of the
    # option to t = 0.
    effective_discount_factor_uix: NDArray[np.int32]
```
</file>
